| Certificates/inventory (§§12.1–12.3) | Closure-keyed device-region records; current text does not explicitly delimit native inventory. | Insert the shared scoping rule in §12.3, cross-reference from §9.2, and require complete device-owner/link-proof coverage per root. Multiple device roots can share static closures without merging root-relative contexts. |
| Launch/entry (§10) | bennett_run(program, cc_entry, args, entropy) starts device CC; it is not native main. | Publish a host call/return shim and container/root selection/feature negotiation. Define native argument/result marshalling, including ptr (whose host binding §10 does not specify), status/fault propagation, suspend/reverse/close and ownership. Do not invent a current general launch shim. |
| Budgets (§5.4; §§10–11) | Validated token tuples and identity scoped to a program/run; action_call has no primary token (§4.7). | Bind selected device invocation budgets and token equality to the launch request; charge actual device/replay work. State host/shim accounting separately; a native process has no invented global B. |
| Handles (§3.3; §10; dialect §6.6) | Register ids refer to the loaded program's ledger; run ownership and token validity are explicit. | Map host-visible opaque handles to that program/ledger with validity and cross-launch ownership checks; preserve class vectors and token identities. Do not serialize native pointers as device ids or token tuples as identities. |
| Effect tape (§7.3; §10) | Run-associated R effects and explicit copied hostcall requests; no implicit log of native host I/O. | Associate each device run and tape with the launched root and define host effect/proxy ordering and replay at the boundary, preserving §7.4 reversal. Saved-journal interchange needs its own versioned replay profile. |
| Host memory by reference (§§7.1–7.2; dialect §§8.5, 17.1, 19.4–19.5) | Device layout plus dialect snapshot/replay and boundary-address/promotion rules; copied hostcall bytes do not provide general shared pointers. | Publish the mapping and lifetime protocol named in the statement, including allocator metadata/history and pointer-rich layouts, so ordinary host memory can be supplied safely without universal host promotion. Retain existing snapshot/replay as a construction, not the sole eventual capability. |

For example native main can call marked roots A and B in distinct ISA objects.
Each has its own object/root certificate and root context []; neither
certificate inventories main or host-only libc. A shared linked device
callee is checked in each root's actual invocation tree. A native comparison
executes the corresponding materialised region/callee closure under its
recorded layout, input and tool configuration (dialect §§17.7, 19.5, 19.9).

These notes specify boundary scope and exact remaining contract work; they
implement no container, host shim or memory registration. The existing
bennett-4ht issue tracks that publication/implementation obligation. Current
device contracts and their complete estimate/proof requirements remain in
force; an unimplemented boundary is not evidence that mixed execution works.


# The document under review (round 1 snapshot)

# b-isa-spec-2qc — checker duty for qinplace coverage

Council proposal. References: [ISA 0.9](../../../spec/Bennett-ISA-Spec-0.9.md)
§4.5 K4, §§9.2–9.3, §12.1 “Invocation contexts”, §12.3 “Invocation
binding (K13)”, §16.3 example 7; related
[0jq owner proposal](../2026-11-02-pass2/b-isa-spec-0jq.md).

## Exact defect and North Star scope

§4.5 already requires an inverse-pair entry in the certificate record
of the region whose body contains qinplace. An unordered pair of exact
g/ginv closure hashes covers either orientation. Coverage also requires
entry.budget ≤ tk componentwise, exact token-argument tuples and
membership of the other arguments in the claim's args domain. The
executor must preflight every transitively executed qinplace before
any effect of a CC/R region call, or trap/fault 4.

§12.1 specifies verification of an entry's claim, and §12.3 specifies
binding resolution, but neither explicitly turns **coverage of sites**
into a checker duty. Validating a supplied inverse-pair proof and
checking that every applicable instruction has one are different tasks.
The request exposes this checker/executor responsibility ambiguity,
not a missing executor trap, inverse algorithm or proof owner. Resolving
all three questions needs static pair presence, bound applicability and
nested-context scope, rather than just “check inverse_pairs”.

North Star §§3.1, 4.2, 5.1 promises checked in-place bijections and honest
proof/evidence labels. Requiring those existing obligations at certificate
acceptance preserves that promise. A standalone proof with unresolved
call parameters may remain useful; it must not be labeled executable
acceptance or be rejected merely for lacking a schedule it never claims.

## Alternatives and concrete witnesses

Use §16.3 example 7, owner `addya`, with qinplace G/GINV, token tk and
classical argument a. The entry proves both compositions on the full
x,y domain, minimum tuple [3,0,0,0], a in 0..3.

| Case | Proposed checker result |
|---|---|
| No entry, or only {G,G} for a {G,GINV} site | Reject missing owner-local pair. |
| tk=[3,0,0,0], a=1, valid full-domain claim | Covered. |
| tk=[9,0,0,0], same a | Covered by primary-token monotonicity. |
| tk=[2,0,0,0] | Reject applicability for that bound invocation. |
| a=3 but the claim covers only 0..2 | Reject applicability. |
| G/GINV swapped in the entry | Covered by the same two-composition proof. |
| Correct entry, no resolvable standalone invocation | Report coverage unestablished; no executable-acceptance conclusion. |

For nesting, outer calls inner twice; inner owns the qinplace. Its tk
actuals are 3 then 2. An entry with minimum 3 covers the first context
and misses the second, even if both have the same variant/control count
and outer's root budget is 9. An entry in outer's claims array cannot
replace inner's. If a qinplace forwards a token argument k, an entry's
arg_tokens.k=[3,0,0,0] does not cover k=[4,0,0,0], however large tk is.

| Alternative | Consequence |
|---|---|
| Executor-only coverage | Safe execution still traps, but a checker can advertise an unusable certificate; requires an explicit limited verdict. |
| Check pair presence and root only | Catches common errors, misses the second inner context above. |
| Check owner presence plus every bound context | Aligns checker acceptance and existing preflight; recommended. |
| Require a universal proof for all possible calls of every standalone record | Unnecessarily rejects useful invocation-scoped/parameter-uniform claims. |

## Recommended exact spec diff

Insert after §12.3 Invocation binding's paragraph ending “not a target
realisation”, before CLAIM-BINDING witness:

```text
Qinplace coverage checking. For every qinplace source site in a supplied
region record, the checker MUST require an inverse_pairs entry in that
owner region's record for the exact pair of resolved g/ginv closure
hashes, in either orientation, and MUST reject a missing pair or invalid
referenced claim. Resolve imported owner records and library claims by
§9.3; indices name claims in their owner record, never in an outer caller.

When a root invocation is bound by bindings.invocation (or by a
standalone claim whose subject is that root), the checker MUST apply
§4.5's complete
covering test at every reached qinplace site in that invocation and its
transitive invocation contexts, rejecting any uncovered site. With a
schedule, use §12.1's complete realised invocation tree, including
default-covered calls, iterations and source-adjoint/control contexts;
without a schedule, use the bound reference invocation tree. Resolve
the site's tk and args from that context, not the root's tuple reused
for all callees. A disabled coherent predicate does not remove an
entered instruction's obligation. Entry claims must be established by
their specified exhaustive or linked-library rule; sampled agreement
does not cover. Do not treat two private generated oracle calls as new
owners of the containing source site's entry.

A standalone record with an entry but no resolvable invocation may
retain its established standalone claims. The checker MUST report its
qinplace applicability as unestablished and MUST NOT conclude executable
acceptance from it. Universal applicability may instead be established
by a checked parameter-uniform argument covering every admitted call.
If a required invocation, linked body or context cannot be resolved or
checked, report that obligation unestablished; an implementation work
limit is not proof of absence or applicability. Malformed bindings and
missing required pairs still reject. No successful verdict may imply
that an unestablished obligation was discharged.

These checks do not remove the executor's §4.5 pre-effect trap/fault
requirement. A verified result may be reused only for the closure,
entry proof and invocation/domain it actually covers; closure equality
alone does not establish applicability at another token/args binding.
```

“Every source site” makes the existing carried-entry requirement explicit
even for a zero-iteration source loop. Context-dependent applicability
is tested only at reached sites. This separates static inventory from
call-dependent coverage; it does not require executing an unreachable
quantum branch as a classical branch or searching all possible CC inputs.
The driver/executor supplies the actual root binding before use.

## Hashes, catalog and consumers

No source envelope, public signature, ref catalog or instruction expansion
changes. Correcting a missing entry changes certificate bytes and any
certificate digest. Wrongly accepted executable packets may now reject
or become explicitly incomplete; this is a compatibility decision, not
an invisible checker patch. Composition must retain each owner's claims
and root-relative contexts; a cache keyed only by closure cannot cache
call coverage. VM, specialiser, producer and independent checker owners
need the same applicability vectors. Fault code 4 and pre-effect timing
are already normative and do not change.

## Conformance vectors and actual verification

Ran the existing independent replay's `QinplaceCoverage` (eight tests)
with K13Witness (four), using [INDEX](INDEX.md)'s bounded command: exit 0,
12 passed. These exercise absent/wrong pairs, swapped orientation,
larger/shorter tk, args range and unbound handling. They do not exercise
the proposed complete nested traversal. The replay explicitly reports
non-root applicability unchecked. Its verdict is not a newly ratified
contract. The published checker's `docs/checker.md` separately states
that verification-index shapes do not discharge qinplace applicability.

Acceptance adds the two-inner-context witness, default-covered and
adjoint calls, qloop 0 versus repeated visits, inherited coherent control,
outer-only entry rejection, imported owner/library records, exact token
argument mismatch and sampled-claim noncoverage. For the executor check
fault 4 occurs before any region effect and preserves the reversible
prefix. A valid structural-only standalone claim must survive with
coverage explicitly unestablished. The finite INDEX predicate checks
the binding distinctions, not a new complete certificate checker.

## K entry and publication route

Recommend an explicit **0.9 K checker-obligation repair**, coordinated
with 0jq, if council authorizes the existing repair track. Proposed row:
`K(new) | §§4.5,12.3 | QINPLACE-CHECKER-COVERAGE: owner-local pair
presence plus all bound transitive contexts; unbound applicability is
unestablished; executor preflight retained | 2qc,0jq | checker semantics`.
Reserve no numeric K identifier. Under the published semantic-version
rule this belongs in **0.10** unless the council explicitly authorizes
an in-version repair; see [INDEX](INDEX.md). Keep 2qc open for that
decision and consumer implementations.

## Mixed fat object

[fat-object-scope.md](../2026-11-02-rev2/fat-object-scope.md) applies (North
Star §§2, 3.3, 4.4). Coverage begins at each selected embedded device
root, context [], and uses its linked owner records. Two native calls to
that root can bind different tk/args; neither inherits coverage merely
from the other's certificate. Native main, host-only libc and other
device roots need no artificial region inventory. The container-to-root
association, launch budget/handle negotiation and tape/run association
remain the exact boundary changes named in the shared note. This proposal
checks device invocations without requiring whole-binary lowering.

# Beads

Use Beads as the shared project task system. Local plans, scratch files, and personal memories are useful, but they are not the durable source of truth for project work.

## First Step

Run:

```bash
bd prime
```

If that prints nothing, check whether the repository has an active Beads workspace:

```bash
bd where
```

## Preferred Route

Use the `bd` CLI when shell access is available. It is the most compact and direct Beads interface.

## Core CLI Workflow

1. Find work:

```bash
bd ready
bd list --status=open
bd list --status=in_progress
```

2. Inspect before editing:

```bash
bd show <id>
```

3. Claim work atomically:

```bash
bd update <id> --claim
```

4. Create durable follow-up work when implementation reveals new tasks:

```bash
bd create "Short title" --description="Why this exists and what needs to be done" --type=task --priority=2
```

5. Close completed work:

```bash
bd close <id> --reason="Completed"
```

## What Belongs In Beads

Use Beads for:

- shared project tasks
- blockers and dependencies
- discovered follow-up work
- work that must survive thread reset, compaction, or handoff
- status that another person or agent should be able to resume

Use agent-local planning tools only for the current turn's execution checklist. Do not treat them as shared project state.

## Rules

- Do not create markdown TODO files as the source of truth when Beads is available.
- Do not use `bd edit`; it opens an interactive editor. Use `bd update` flags instead.
- Prefer `--json` when parsing `bd` output programmatically.
- If hooks are installed, `bd prime` may already be injected. Run it manually when context is missing.
- Do not auto-close or mutate tasks unless the work is actually complete.

1003:| `qinplace` | `qinplace @g @ginv tk x (ins..) (mem..) (args..)`; written `qinplace @g @ginv tk x` when all three lists are empty (and only then) | **Well-formed** iff `@g` and `@ginv` are oracles whose `in` slots are x's width n followed by the widths of `ins` in order, whose `tgt` width is n, whose fault widths are equal (F), whose `mem` slots match the `mem` regions (role `in`, §3.3's matching rule) and whose `args` types are those of `args`; x, the `ins` wires and the effective control context are pairwise disjoint. **Sem**: `t = qalloc any n; f = qalloc any F; oracle_call @g tk (x, ins..) t f (mem..) (args..); qexch x t; oracle_call @ginv tk (x, ins..) t f (mem..) (args..); qfree t; qfree f`, so x ← g(x; ins, mem, args); these are its generated operations, in this order (§11). **Inverse-pair obligation**: under the tuple of `tk`, the call's `args` values and the tuples of the tokens among them, on every basis value of x, of the `ins` wires and of the `mem` cells, both oracles are fault-free and g(·; ins, mem, args) and ginv(·; ins, mem, args) are mutually inverse permutations of the n-bit encoding. It is carried by an `inverse_pairs` entry (§12.3) of the certificate record of the region whose body contains the `qinplace`; an entry `{g: G, ginv: H}` covers both `qinplace @G @H` and `qinplace @H @G` (its claim checks both compositions). An entry **covers** a `qinplace` when its `budget` is componentwise ≤ the tuple of `tk` (success at a tuple implies the same result at every larger one, §5.4), every token-typed argument's tuple equals the entry's `arg_tokens` tuple for that argument (equality: a nested oracle's outcome may change with its budget), and every other `args` value lies in its claim's `args` domain. Its claim is an `exhaustive` claim with `expected: "inverse_pair"`, which the executor verifies as a checker does (once per closure hash, before first use), or a `structural` `inverse_pair_library` claim, which covers when the named claim of the linked library's certificate is such a claim for the same closure hashes of g and ginv (no re-execution; a library claim without an `args` domain covers every value; its proof or evidence label is the library claim's); a `sampled` claim never covers. Before any effect of a CC/R region call, every `qinplace` the callee executes (transitively; its token and `args` are resolved from the call's classical arguments, which reach lifted calls only as classical arguments, §4.2) MUST have a covering entry, else CC trap / R fault `FAULT_UB_TRAP`. The private t and f are not values of the region for §6: the certified pair cleans them, the only cleanup that is not a §6.4 realisation. Under control the allocations are unconditional and every gate of both calls and of the exchange is controlled |

# b-isa-spec-2qc — checker duty for qinplace coverage

Council proposal. References: [ISA 0.9](../../../spec/Bennett-ISA-Spec-0.9.md)
§4.5 K4, §§9.2–9.3, §12.1 “Invocation contexts”, §12.3 “Invocation
binding (K13)”, §16.3 example 7; related
[0jq owner proposal](../2026-11-02-pass2/b-isa-spec-0jq.md).

## Exact defect and North Star scope

§4.5 already requires an inverse-pair entry in the certificate record
of the region whose body contains qinplace. An unordered pair of exact
g/ginv closure hashes covers either orientation. Coverage also requires
entry.budget ≤ tk componentwise, exact token-argument tuples and
membership of the other arguments in the claim's args domain. The
executor must preflight every transitively executed qinplace before
any effect of a CC/R region call, or trap/fault 4.

§12.1 specifies verification of an entry's claim, and §12.3 specifies
binding resolution, but neither explicitly turns **coverage of sites**
into a checker duty. Validating a supplied inverse-pair proof and
checking that every applicable instruction has one are different tasks.
The request exposes this checker/executor responsibility ambiguity,
not a missing executor trap, inverse algorithm or proof owner. Resolving
all three questions needs static pair presence, bound applicability and
nested-context scope, rather than just “check inverse_pairs”.

North Star §§3.1, 4.2, 5.1 promises checked in-place bijections and honest
proof/evidence labels. Requiring those existing obligations at certificate
acceptance preserves that promise. A standalone proof with unresolved
call parameters may remain useful; it must not be labeled executable
acceptance or be rejected merely for lacking a schedule it never claims.

## Alternatives and concrete witnesses

Use §16.3 example 7, owner `addya`, with qinplace G/GINV, token tk and
classical argument a. The entry proves both compositions on the full
x,y domain, minimum tuple [3,0,0,0], a in 0..3.

| Case | Proposed checker result |
|---|---|
| No entry, or only {G,G} for a {G,GINV} site | Reject missing owner-local pair. |
| tk=[3,0,0,0], a=1, valid full-domain claim | Covered. |
| tk=[9,0,0,0], same a | Covered by primary-token monotonicity. |
| tk=[2,0,0,0] | Reject applicability for that bound invocation. |
| a=3 but the claim covers only 0..2 | Reject applicability. |
| G/GINV swapped in the entry | Covered by the same two-composition proof. |
| Correct entry, no resolvable standalone invocation | Report coverage unestablished; no executable-acceptance conclusion. |

For nesting, outer calls inner twice; inner owns the qinplace. Its tk
actuals are 3 then 2. An entry with minimum 3 covers the first context
and misses the second, even if both have the same variant/control count
and outer's root budget is 9. An entry in outer's claims array cannot
replace inner's. If a qinplace forwards a token argument k, an entry's
arg_tokens.k=[3,0,0,0] does not cover k=[4,0,0,0], however large tk is.

| Alternative | Consequence |
|---|---|
| Executor-only coverage | Safe execution still traps, but a checker can advertise an unusable certificate; requires an explicit limited verdict. |
| Check pair presence and root only | Catches common errors, misses the second inner context above. |
| Check owner presence plus every bound context | Aligns checker acceptance and existing preflight; recommended. |
| Require a universal proof for all possible calls of every standalone record | Unnecessarily rejects useful invocation-scoped/parameter-uniform claims. |

## Recommended exact spec diff

Insert after §12.3 Invocation binding's paragraph ending “not a target
realisation”, before CLAIM-BINDING witness:

```text
Qinplace coverage checking. For every qinplace source site in a supplied
region record, the checker MUST require an inverse_pairs entry in that
owner region's record for the exact pair of resolved g/ginv closure
hashes, in either orientation, and MUST reject a missing pair or invalid
referenced claim. Resolve imported owner records and library claims by
§9.3; indices name claims in their owner record, never in an outer caller.

When a root invocation is bound by bindings.invocation (or by a
standalone claim whose subject is that root), the checker MUST apply
§4.5's complete
covering test at every reached qinplace site in that invocation and its
transitive invocation contexts, rejecting any uncovered site. With a
schedule, use §12.1's complete realised invocation tree, including
default-covered calls, iterations and source-adjoint/control contexts;
without a schedule, use the bound reference invocation tree. Resolve
the site's tk and args from that context, not the root's tuple reused
for all callees. A disabled coherent predicate does not remove an
entered instruction's obligation. Entry claims must be established by
their specified exhaustive or linked-library rule; sampled agreement
does not cover. Do not treat two private generated oracle calls as new
owners of the containing source site's entry.

A standalone record with an entry but no resolvable invocation may
retain its established standalone claims. The checker MUST report its
qinplace applicability as unestablished and MUST NOT conclude executable
acceptance from it. Universal applicability may instead be established
by a checked parameter-uniform argument covering every admitted call.
If a required invocation, linked body or context cannot be resolved or
checked, report that obligation unestablished; an implementation work
limit is not proof of absence or applicability. Malformed bindings and
missing required pairs still reject. No successful verdict may imply
that an unestablished obligation was discharged.

These checks do not remove the executor's §4.5 pre-effect trap/fault
requirement. A verified result may be reused only for the closure,
entry proof and invocation/domain it actually covers; closure equality
alone does not establish applicability at another token/args binding.
```

“Every source site” makes the existing carried-entry requirement explicit
even for a zero-iteration source loop. Context-dependent applicability
is tested only at reached sites. This separates static inventory from
call-dependent coverage; it does not require executing an unreachable
quantum branch as a classical branch or searching all possible CC inputs.
The driver/executor supplies the actual root binding before use.

## Hashes, catalog and consumers

No source envelope, public signature, ref catalog or instruction expansion
changes. Correcting a missing entry changes certificate bytes and any
certificate digest. Wrongly accepted executable packets may now reject
or become explicitly incomplete; this is a compatibility decision, not
an invisible checker patch. Composition must retain each owner's claims
and root-relative contexts; a cache keyed only by closure cannot cache
call coverage. VM, specialiser, producer and independent checker owners
need the same applicability vectors. Fault code 4 and pre-effect timing
are already normative and do not change.

## Conformance vectors and actual verification

Ran the existing independent replay's `QinplaceCoverage` (eight tests)
with K13Witness (four), using [INDEX](INDEX.md)'s bounded command: exit 0,
12 passed. These exercise absent/wrong pairs, swapped orientation,
larger/shorter tk, args range and unbound handling. They do not exercise
the proposed complete nested traversal. The replay explicitly reports
non-root applicability unchecked. Its verdict is not a newly ratified
contract. The published checker's `docs/checker.md` separately states
that verification-index shapes do not discharge qinplace applicability.

Acceptance adds the two-inner-context witness, default-covered and
adjoint calls, qloop 0 versus repeated visits, inherited coherent control,
outer-only entry rejection, imported owner/library records, exact token
argument mismatch and sampled-claim noncoverage. For the executor check
fault 4 occurs before any region effect and preserves the reversible
prefix. A valid structural-only standalone claim must survive with
coverage explicitly unestablished. The finite INDEX predicate checks
the binding distinctions, not a new complete certificate checker.

## K entry and publication route

Recommend an explicit **0.9 K checker-obligation repair**, coordinated
with 0jq, if council authorizes the existing repair track. Proposed row:
`K(new) | §§4.5,12.3 | QINPLACE-CHECKER-COVERAGE: owner-local pair
presence plus all bound transitive contexts; unbound applicability is
unestablished; executor preflight retained | 2qc,0jq | checker semantics`.
Reserve no numeric K identifier. Under the published semantic-version
rule this belongs in **0.10** unless the council explicitly authorizes
an in-version repair; see [INDEX](INDEX.md). Keep 2qc open for that
decision and consumer implementations.

## Mixed fat object

[fat-object-scope.md](../2026-11-02-rev2/fat-object-scope.md) applies (North
Star §§2, 3.3, 4.4). Coverage begins at each selected embedded device
root, context [], and uses its linked owner records. Two native calls to
that root can bind different tk/args; neither inherits coverage merely
from the other's certificate. Native main, host-only libc and other
device roots need no artificial region inventory. The container-to-root
association, launch budget/handle negotiation and tape/run association
remain the exact boundary changes named in the shared note. This proposal
checks device invocations without requiring whole-binary lowering.

Five proposals, not published spec changes or consumer acceptance. Read

for 4yg.1 and brt-vbi. No passive JSONL was imported or substituted for

| Issue | Determination | Recommended publication |
|---|---|---|
| [bennett-arith-4yg.1](bennett-arith-4yg.1.md) | Valid FMA and binary128-conversion midpoints have no even candidate under the printed PPC rule; flags separately violate an existing rule | Dialect §13.1 parity repair in next 0.2 patch and 0.3 draft; no ISA K |
| [brt-vbi](brt-vbi.md) | Published all-width helper requirements lack a public delivery/collection protocol; actual i257 bodies also missing | Dialect §§12.8/19.1 offline interface and fixed point; no new ISA opcode/K |

| [b-isa-spec-2qc](b-isa-spec-2qc.md) | Executor preflight and proof owner exist; certificate checker coverage duty is ambiguous | Explicit owner/site/context rule, coordinated with 0jq; council-authorized 0.9 K repair or 0.10 |
| [b-isa-spec-sqr](b-isa-spec-sqr.md) | Bundle entries in an mcx list have no explicit operand rule; current implementations disagree | Ordered bundle expansion, full wire arity/distinctness; council-authorized 0.9 K repair or 0.10 |

None is an excuse to reduce a format/width domain, omit an inverse
obligation, or declare a North Star capability inert. K13 is an example
defect rather than a new machine capability gap. The remaining findings
concern deterministic contract interpretation or delivery. All existing
SQLite, Julia, libc, reversible execution and demo-ladder obligations
remain. Existing beads and cross-repo requests already track the decisions;
no duplicate task or sibling tracker mutation is needed for this pass.

## Publication and combined decisions

[spec/README.md](../../../spec/README.md) requires a full version change
for semantic changes and council review. “0.9 K repair” in these notes
means a proposed use of the existing council repair process, **not
authority to silently amend a published contract**. Without explicit
in-version authorization, the 2qc/sqr semantic additions go to 0.10.
New version prefixes change affected object/interface/closure envelopes,
example identities and consumer pins. A same-version repair with unchanged
source bytes preserves their source identities but still invalidates
incompatible execution/schedule/proof assumptions. `ref@0.9` changes only
if its own catalog bytes/version change; none of these proposals requires
such a rewrite. Assign numeric K entries only at acceptance.

PPC parity and runtime delivery belong to the dialect because they define
LLVM numerical/helper lowering and build inputs. ISA 0.9/0.10 need no
new K for either. Publish the exact proposed dialect text in the next
available maintained patch and mirror it in 0.3; the proposal is not a
private runtime/driver agreement. The new helper protocol is itself
versioned with schema 1, independent of ISA subformat numbers.

For 2qc use the existing concrete invocation-context vocabulary, owner
closure selection and owner-local claim indices. Coordinate with
[pass2 0jq](../2026-11-02-pass2/b-isa-spec-0jq.md); do not create a second
inverse proof owner or replace tuple applicability by root budget equality.
For sqr retain the written source identity and resolve complete wire
operands before K78 primitive matching and scheduling. A parenthesized
indexed list is not currently a legal substitute under §16.2.

## Mixed fat object

Every note cites and applies
[fat-object-scope.md](../2026-11-02-rev2/fat-object-scope.md), binding under
bennett-4ht and advisor 08:45Z (North Star §§2, 3.3, 4.4). A native host
binary remains native; only marked device modules and their required
linked closures become ISA objects. Helper generation, comparison,
certificate inventory and invocation coverage bind those device roots,
with a separate root-relative context [] per root. They do not inventory
native main, host-only argv/envp setup or host-only libc. The shared note
names the exact remaining container/root/certificate association, launch,
budget/handle, tape/run and host-memory mapping changes. This pass neither
implements those mechanisms nor locks them out.

```python
from pathlib import Path
from fractions import Fraction as F
from itertools import product
import hashlib, json, os, re, subprocess, sys

ROOT = Path('/home/tobias/Projects/bennett-workspace/bennett-isa-spec')
WS = ROOT.parent
OUT = ROOT / 'docs/notes/2026-11-02-derive3'
JOB = ROOT / '.tmp/sol-derive3'
REF = JOB / 'target/debug/bennett-isa-ref'
REPLAY = WS / 'bennett-conformance/checker-replay'
WITNESS = REPLAY / 'witnesses/mcx-bundle-control'
assert os.environ['TMPDIR'] == str(JOB / 'tmp')
assert os.environ['CARGO_TARGET_DIR'] == str(JOB / 'target')
assert not any(k in os.environ for k in
               ('DISPLAY', 'WAYLAND_DISPLAY', 'DBUS_SESSION_BUS_ADDRESS', 'BROWSER'))
ids = ['bennett-arith-4yg.1', 'brt-vbi', 'b-isa-spec-pe1',
       'b-isa-spec-2qc', 'b-isa-spec-sqr']
assert {p.name for p in OUT.iterdir()} == {i+'.md' for i in ids} | {'INDEX.md'}
index = (OUT / 'INDEX.md').read_text()
for ident in ids:
    s = (OUT / (ident+'.md')).read_text()
    assert ident in s and not re.search(r'\b(?:TODO|TBD)\b', s)
    for heading in ('defect', 'north star', 'alternatives', 'recommended exact',
                    'hashes', 'conformance', 'k entry', 'mixed fat object'):
        assert heading in s.lower(), (ident, heading)
    assert 'fat-object-scope.md' in s
    assert index.count('| ['+ident+']') == 1
for p in OUT.glob('*.md'):
    s = p.read_text()
    assert sum(line.startswith('```') for line in s.splitlines()) % 2 == 0, p
    prose = re.sub(r'```[^\n]*\n.*?\n```', '', s, flags=re.S)
    for link in re.findall(r'\[[^\]]+\]\(([^)]+)\)', prose):
        if not link.startswith(('http://', 'https://', '#')):
            assert (p.parent / link.split('#')[0]).exists(), (p, link)
print('PASS: five proposals and index; sections, links, fences, fat-object citations')

def pow2(n):
    return F(2**n) if n >= 0 else F(1, 2**(-n))
def decode(bits, fraction_bits, exponent_bits, bias):
    sign = -1 if bits >> (fraction_bits+exponent_bits) else 1
    exp = (bits >> fraction_bits) & ((1 << exponent_bits)-1)
    frac = bits & ((1 << fraction_bits)-1)
    assert exp != (1 << exponent_bits)-1
    sig = frac if exp == 0 else (1 << fraction_bits) | frac
    return sign * sig * pow2((1-bias if exp == 0 else exp-bias)-fraction_bits)
def f64(bits):
    return decode(bits, 52, 11, 1023)
u = pow2(-1074)
h = f64(0x3ff0000000000001)
assert h == 1+pow2(-52)
exact = (h+u)*F(1,2) + f64(0x3fe0000000000001)
assert exact == h+u/2
H = f64(0x0030000000000001)
V = decode(0x3c030000000000001200000000000000, 112, 15, 16383)
assert V == H+u/2 and H > pow2(-1022)
for hi, v in ((h, exact), (H, V)):
    assert (hi/u).denominator == 1
    assert v-hi == hi+u-v == u/2
    assert hi+u-hi == u
# The two high raw significands and the nonzero low significand are odd.
assert (0x3ff0000000000001 & 1) == (0x0030000000000001 & 1) == 1
assert (0 & 1, 1 & 1) == (0, 1)  # proposed common low comparison selects zero
# Both-low-zero subnormal neighbours still use high parity.
assert f64(2)-f64(1) == u and (1 & 1, 2 & 1) == (1, 0)
print('PASS: independent exact PPC FMA/conversion midpoints and parity distinction')

spec = (ROOT / 'spec/Bennett-ISA-Spec-0.9.md').read_text()
block = spec.split('**CLAIM-REPLAY witness**', 1)[1].split('```json\n', 1)[1].split('\n```', 1)[0]
assert block.count('"borrowed":["1"]') == 6
fixed = block.replace('"borrowed":["1"]', '"borrowed":[]')
fixed = fixed.replace('"borrowed":[1]', '"borrowed":[]')
fixed = fixed.replace('1/33554432', '1/16777216')
k13 = json.loads(fixed)
tuples = [k13['row']['before'], k13['row']['after'],
          k13['wrong_row']['before'], k13['wrong_row']['after'],
          k13['changed_arg_after'], k13['short_budget_after']]
assert all(t['borrowed'] == [] and t['regions'] == [['2','1']] for t in tuples)
assert k13['widths']['borrowed'] == []
bits = sum(k13['widths']['bundles']) + sum(sum(r) for r in k13['widths']['regions'])
assert bits == 24 and str(F(1, 1 << bits)) == k13['covered_fraction']
assert 2 ^ ((1+2) % 4) == 1 and 2 ^ ((1+1) % 4) == 0
assert (2 ^ 0, 3 ^ 1) == (2, 2)
assert k13['sample_count'] == 4 and k13['distinct_samples'] == 1
print('PASS: all six K13 tuple repairs; 24-bit domain and exact singleton coverage')

# A finite coverage counterexample, not an implementation of the checker.
minimum = (3,0,0,0)
def covers(tk, arg, domain=range(4), token_arg=None):
    return (all(a <= b for a,b in zip(minimum,tk)) and arg in domain
            and (token_arg is None or token_arg == (3,0,0,0)))
assert covers((3,0,0,0),1) and covers((9,0,0,0),1)
assert not covers((2,0,0,0),1)
assert not covers((3,0,0,0),3,range(3))
assert not covers((9,0,0,0),1,token_arg=(4,0,0,0))
contexts = [(3,0,0,0), (2,0,0,0)]
assert [covers(t,1) for t in contexts] == [True,False]
assert {('G','H'), ('H','G')} == {('H','G'), ('G','H')}
print('PASS: qinplace minimum, exact-token, args-domain and two-context counterexamples')

# Proposed wire-request example; arithmetic is independent of any runtime body.

assert a.bit_count() == 3 and (((a*b) & mask).bit_count()) == 2
assert [min(64,257-64*k) for k in range(5)] == [64,64,64,64,1]
entries = [{'op':'mul','width':257}, {'op':'ctpop','width':257}, {'op':'mul','width':257}]

assert canonical_entries == [{'op':'ctpop','width':257},{'op':'mul','width':257}]
request = {'schema':'bennett-runtimes-instantiations-1', 'llvm_major':18,
           'target_triple':'x86_64-unknown-linux-musl', 'data_layout':'e-p:64:64',
           'entries':canonical_entries}

print('PASS: i257 top-limb arithmetic and canonical request example')

def run(argv, cwd=ROOT, timeout=20, expected=0):
    p = subprocess.run([str(x) for x in argv], cwd=cwd, text=True,
                       capture_output=True, timeout=timeout)
    assert p.returncode == expected, (argv,p.returncode,p.stdout,p.stderr)
    return p
native = run([sys.executable,'-I','-B','-u',WS/'bennett-arith/tests/ppc_zero_low_witness.py'])

assert native.stdout.count('flags=24;') == 2
print(native.stdout, end='')

             'tests.test_replay.K13Witness','tests.test_replay.QinplaceCoverage'],cwd=REPLAY)

print('PASS: independent replay focused tests, 12/12')
runtime = WS/'bennett-runtimes'

    tools = Path(f'/usr/lib/llvm-{major}/bin')
    run([tools/'opt','-passes=verify','-disable-output',ll])

    defs = {line.split()[-1] for line in nm.splitlines() if line.split()}
    assert not {'__bennett_rt_i257_mul','__bennett_rt_i257_ctpop'} & defs
    print('PASS: LLVM',major,'i257 source verifies; both definitions absent;',
          'runtime SHA-256',hashlib.sha256(bc.read_bytes()).hexdigest())

replay = json.loads(run([sys.executable,'-B','-m','checker_replay',
                        WITNESS/'certificate.json','--object',WITNESS/'object.bisa',
                        '--max-elements','16'],cwd=REPLAY).stdout)

assert (claim['outcome'],claim['elements'],claim['covered_fraction']) == ('proof',16,'1')
original = run([REF,'check',WITNESS/'object.bisa'],expected=1)
assert 'c must name one wire' in original.stderr
scratch = JOB/'tmp/mcx-ccx.bisa'
source = (WITNESS/'object.bisa').read_text()
old = 'e38d5da64f9a0ae89a66243bcbe5a321cb807a5bbabf83952b63d3465dfab346'

scratch.write_text(source.replace('mcx (c) p','ccx c[0] c[1] p').replace(old,new))

    result = json.loads(run([REF,command,scratch]).stdout)
    assert result['object_hash'] == 'c785ff3f310f968e2a26f68834e99ab892cf0d1bffe816cf26b17d45c33a65af'
    assert result['symbols']['a']['interface_hash'] == new
    assert result['symbols']['a']['closure_hash'] == new
for p,q,c in product(range(2),range(2),range(4)):
    result = json.loads(run([REF,'run-action',scratch,'a','--mut',f'{p},{q}','--in',str(c)]).stdout)
    assert result['mut'] == [q ^ int(c==3),p] and result['in'] == [c]
    back = json.loads(run([REF,'run-action',scratch,'a','--mut',
                          ','.join(map(str,result['mut'])),'--in',str(c),'--adjoint']).stdout)
    assert back['mut'] == [p,q] and back['in'] == [c]
print('PASS: mcx replay proof; original reference rejection; ccx check/hash and 16+16 executions')
print('PASS: derive3 verification complete; proposals remain unaccepted/unimplemented')

## Remaining decisions and ownership

Council/editor owners must ratify and publish these changes; the existing
five issues remain their tracking records. Arith still owes exact incoming
conversion/FMA mechanics and flags after the parity decision. Runtimes
and driver/plugin still owe the helper protocol and bodies. Checker and
executor owners must add the proposed context/operand vectors, and the
K13 editor must publish a complete corrected fixture. None of that
downstream implementation is claimed complete by this notes-only job.
# spec/

`Bennett-ISA-Spec.md` is the normative text: draft 0.8, **accepted by the
three-model council on 2026-10-28 (round 8, all members accept, no blocking
issues, mean 4.17/5)**. Council records and the runner live in the `bennett`
meta repo (`docs/council/isa-2026-10-28/`, `tools/council.py`).

Rules for changing it:

- Every semantic change bumps the full version string (section 1); drafts
  are not envelope-compatible, so the four example hashes in section 16.3
  change with it. Run `python3 reference-interpreter/isa_check.py --repatch`
  then `python3 reference-interpreter/isa_check.py`.
- Surgical edits only; a rewrite has introduced a mechanical error every
  time it was tried.
- Add every new example or stated result to `isa_check.py` before anyone
  grades it.
- Section 17 lists decisions deferred to implementation with their
  defaults; an implementer who must choose something not covered there
  files an issue and the default goes into section 17 first.
- Revisions go back through the council.

# 🚨 SESSION CLOSE PROTOCOL 🚨

**CRITICAL**: Before saying "done" or "complete", you MUST run this checklist:

```
[ ] 1. bd close <id1> <id2> ...   (close completed issues)
[ ] 2. run quality gates        (tests, linters, builds when relevant)
[ ] 3. git status               (check what changed)
[ ] 4. follow active profile    (conservative: report handoff; team-maintainer: commit/sync/push if enabled)
```

**Policy:** Conservative is the default. Commit, sync, or push only when the active user, orchestrator, or repository profile grants that authority.

## Core Rules
- **Default**: Use beads for ALL task tracking (`bd create`, `bd ready`, `bd close`)
- **Prohibited**: Do NOT use TodoWrite, TaskCreate, or markdown files for task tracking
- **Workflow**: Create beads issue BEFORE writing code, mark in_progress when starting
- **Memory**: Use `bd remember "insight"` for persistent knowledge across sessions. Do NOT use MEMORY.md files — they fragment across accounts. Search with `bd memories <keyword>`.
- Persistence you don't need beats lost context
- Default: do not commit, push, or run dolt remote sync without explicit authority. Team-maintainer behavior is opt-in and still subordinate to user/orchestrator instructions.
- Git workflow: conservative by default; commit/push only with explicit user/orchestrator or team-maintainer authority
- Session management: check `bd ready` for available work

## Essential Commands

### Finding Work
- `bd ready` - Show issues ready to work (no blockers)
- `bd list --status=open` - All open issues
- `bd list --status=in_progress` - Your active work
- `bd show <id>` - Detailed issue view with dependencies

### Creating & Updating
- `bd create --title="Summary of this issue" --description="Why this issue exists and what needs to be done" --type=task|bug|feature --priority=2` - New issue
  - Priority: 0-4 or P0-P4 (0=critical, 2=medium, 4=backlog). NOT "high"/"medium"/"low"
- `bd create ... --parent=<id>` - Hierarchical child (task under epic, subtask under task; inherits parent labels)
- `bd update <id> --claim` - Claim work
- `bd unclaim <id>` - Release stuck issue (agent crashed)
- `bd update <id> --assignee=username` - Assign to someone
- `bd update <id> --if-assignee=<expected> --assignee=<new>` - Atomic reassign: applies only if the assignee still matches (--if-status=<expected> guards status; --if-assignee='' requires unassigned). Mismatch exits non-zero with nothing written — never retry blindly
- `bd update <id> --title/--description/--notes/--design` - Update fields inline
- `bd close <id>` - Mark complete
- `bd close <id1> <id2> ...` - Close multiple issues at once (more efficient)
- `bd close <id> --reason="explanation"` - Close with reason
- **Tip**: When creating multiple issues/tasks/epics, use parallel subagents for efficiency
- **WARNING**: Do NOT use `bd edit` - it opens $EDITOR (vim/nano) which blocks agents

### Dependencies & Blocking
- `bd dep add <issue> <depends-on>` - Add dependency (issue depends on depends-on)
- `bd blocked` - Show all blocked issues
- `bd show <id>` - See what's blocking/blocked by this issue

### Sync & Collaboration
- `bd search <query>` - Search issues by keyword

### Project Health
- `bd stats` - Project statistics (open/closed/blocked counts)
- `bd doctor` - Check for issues (sync problems, missing hooks)
- `bd doctor --check=conventions` - Check for convention drift (lint, stale, orphans)

### Quality Tools
- `bd create --validate` - Check description has required sections
- `bd create --acceptance="criteria"` - Set acceptance criteria (checked by --validate)
- `bd create --design="decisions"` - Record design decisions
- `bd create --notes="context"` - Add supplementary notes
- `bd config set validation.on-create warn` - Auto-validate on every create
- `bd lint` - Check existing issues for missing sections

### Lifecycle & Hygiene
- `bd defer <id> --until="date"` - Defer work to a future date
- `bd supersede <id> --with=<new-id>` - Mark issue as superseded
- `bd close <id> --suggest-next` - Show newly unblocked issues after closing
- `bd stale` - Find issues with no recent activity
- `bd orphans` - Find issues with broken dependencies
- `bd preflight` - Pre-PR checks (lint, stale, orphans)
- `bd human <id>` - Flag for human decision (list/respond/dismiss)

### Structured Workflows
- `bd formula list` - See available workflow templates
- `bd mol pour <name>` - Start structured workflow from formula

## Common Workflows

**Starting work:**
```bash
bd ready           # Find available work
bd show <id>       # Review issue details
bd update <id> --claim  # Claim it
```

**Completing work:**
```bash
bd close <id1> <id2> ...    # Close all completed issues at once
git status                  # Check changed files
# Conservative/minimal/default: report status and proposed commands; wait for approval
# Team-maintainer opt-in only, unless current instructions forbid it:
# git add . && git commit -m "..."
# git push
```

**Creating dependent work:**
```bash
# Run bd create commands in parallel (use subagents for many items)
bd create --title="Implement feature X" --description="Why this issue exists and what needs to be done" --type=feature
bd create --title="Write tests for X" --description="Why this issue exists and what needs to be done" --type=task
bd dep add beads-yyy beads-xxx  # Tests depend on Feature (Feature blocks tests)
```

> **Note**: AGENTS.md and CLAUDE.md are independent files (not symlinked and not sharing an inode). Mirror substantive edits across both, or symlink one to the other.

| Issue | Determination | Recommended publication |
|---|---|---|
| [bennett-arith-4yg.1](bennett-arith-4yg.1.md) | Valid FMA and binary128-conversion midpoints have no even candidate under the printed PPC rule; flags separately violate an existing rule | Dialect §13.1 parity repair in next 0.2 patch and 0.3 draft; no ISA K |
| [brt-vbi](brt-vbi.md) | Published all-width helper requirements lack a public delivery/collection protocol; actual i257 bodies also missing | Dialect §§12.8/19.1 offline interface and fixed point; no new ISA opcode/K |
| [b-isa-spec-pe1](b-isa-spec-pe1.md) | K13 example invents a borrowed lifetime that K70 rejects | 0.9 K13 example erratum: empty borrowed arrays, 24-bit D |
| [b-isa-spec-2qc](b-isa-spec-2qc.md) | Executor preflight and proof owner exist; certificate checker coverage duty is ambiguous | Explicit owner/site/context rule, coordinated with 0jq; council-authorized 0.9 K repair or 0.10 |
| [b-isa-spec-sqr](b-isa-spec-sqr.md) | Bundle entries in an mcx list have no explicit operand rule; current implementations disagree | Ordered bundle expansion, full wire arity/distinctness; council-authorized 0.9 K repair or 0.10 |

None is an excuse to reduce a format/width domain, omit an inverse
obligation, or declare a North Star capability inert. K13 is an example
defect rather than a new machine capability gap. The remaining findings

remain. Existing beads and cross-repo requests already track the decisions;
no duplicate task or sibling tracker mutation is needed for this pass.

## Publication and combined decisions

[spec/README.md](../../../spec/README.md) requires a full version change
for semantic changes and council review. “0.9 K repair” in these notes
means a proposed use of the existing council repair process, **not
authority to silently amend a published contract**. Without explicit
in-version authorization, the 2qc/sqr semantic additions go to 0.10.
New version prefixes change affected object/interface/closure envelopes,
example identities and consumer pins. A same-version repair with unchanged
source bytes preserves their source identities but still invalidates
incompatible execution/schedule/proof assumptions. `ref@0.9` changes only
if its own catalog bytes/version change; none of these proposals requires
such a rewrite. Assign numeric K entries only at acceptance.

PPC parity and runtime delivery belong to the dialect because they define
LLVM numerical/helper lowering and build inputs. ISA 0.9/0.10 need no
new K for either. Publish the exact proposed dialect text in the next
available maintained patch and mirror it in 0.3; the proposal is not a
private runtime/driver agreement. The new helper protocol is itself
versioned with schema 1, independent of ISA subformat numbers.

For 2qc use the existing concrete invocation-context vocabulary, owner
closure selection and owner-local claim indices. Coordinate with
[pass2 0jq](../2026-11-02-pass2/b-isa-spec-0jq.md); do not create a second
inverse proof owner or replace tuple applicability by root budget equality.
For sqr retain the written source identity and resolve complete wire
operands before K78 primitive matching and scheduling. A parenthesized
indexed list is not currently a legal substitute under §16.2.

## Mixed fat object

Every note cites and applies
[fat-object-scope.md](../2026-11-02-rev2/fat-object-scope.md), binding under
bennett-4ht and advisor 08:45Z (North Star §§2, 3.3, 4.4). A native host
binary remains native; only marked device modules and their required
linked closures become ISA objects. Helper generation, comparison,
certificate inventory and invocation coverage bind those device roots,
with a separate root-relative context [] per root. They do not inventory
native main, host-only argv/envp setup or host-only libc. The shared note
names the exact remaining container/root/certificate association, launch,
budget/handle, tape/run and host-memory mapping changes. This pass neither
implements those mechanisms nor locks them out.

```python
from pathlib import Path
from fractions import Fraction as F
from itertools import product
import hashlib, json, os, re, subprocess, sys

ROOT = Path('/home/tobias/Projects/bennett-workspace/bennett-isa-spec')
WS = ROOT.parent
OUT = ROOT / 'docs/notes/2026-11-02-derive3'
JOB = ROOT / '.tmp/sol-derive3'
REF = JOB / 'target/debug/bennett-isa-ref'
REPLAY = WS / 'bennett-conformance/checker-replay'
WITNESS = REPLAY / 'witnesses/mcx-bundle-control'
assert os.environ['TMPDIR'] == str(JOB / 'tmp')
assert os.environ['CARGO_TARGET_DIR'] == str(JOB / 'target')
assert not any(k in os.environ for k in
               ('DISPLAY', 'WAYLAND_DISPLAY', 'DBUS_SESSION_BUS_ADDRESS', 'BROWSER'))
ids = ['bennett-arith-4yg.1', 'brt-vbi', 'b-isa-spec-pe1',
       'b-isa-spec-2qc', 'b-isa-spec-sqr']
assert {p.name for p in OUT.iterdir()} == {i+'.md' for i in ids} | {'INDEX.md'}
index = (OUT / 'INDEX.md').read_text()
for ident in ids:
    s = (OUT / (ident+'.md')).read_text()
    assert ident in s and not re.search(r'\b(?:TODO|TBD)\b', s)
    for heading in ('defect', 'north star', 'alternatives', 'recommended exact',
                    'hashes', 'conformance', 'k entry', 'mixed fat object'):
        assert heading in s.lower(), (ident, heading)
    assert 'fat-object-scope.md' in s
    assert index.count('| ['+ident+']') == 1
for p in OUT.glob('*.md'):
    s = p.read_text()
    assert sum(line.startswith('```') for line in s.splitlines()) % 2 == 0, p
    prose = re.sub(r'```[^\n]*\n.*?\n```', '', s, flags=re.S)
    for link in re.findall(r'\[[^\]]+\]\(([^)]+)\)', prose):
        if not link.startswith(('http://', 'https://', '#')):
            assert (p.parent / link.split('#')[0]).exists(), (p, link)
print('PASS: five proposals and index; sections, links, fences, fat-object citations')

def pow2(n):
    return F(2**n) if n >= 0 else F(1, 2**(-n))
def decode(bits, fraction_bits, exponent_bits, bias):
    sign = -1 if bits >> (fraction_bits+exponent_bits) else 1
    exp = (bits >> fraction_bits) & ((1 << exponent_bits)-1)
    frac = bits & ((1 << fraction_bits)-1)
    assert exp != (1 << exponent_bits)-1
    sig = frac if exp == 0 else (1 << fraction_bits) | frac
    return sign * sig * pow2((1-bias if exp == 0 else exp-bias)-fraction_bits)
def f64(bits):
    return decode(bits, 52, 11, 1023)
u = pow2(-1074)
h = f64(0x3ff0000000000001)
assert h == 1+pow2(-52)
exact = (h+u)*F(1,2) + f64(0x3fe0000000000001)
assert exact == h+u/2
H = f64(0x0030000000000001)
V = decode(0x3c030000000000001200000000000000, 112, 15, 16383)
assert V == H+u/2 and H > pow2(-1022)
for hi, v in ((h, exact), (H, V)):
    assert (hi/u).denominator == 1
    assert v-hi == hi+u-v == u/2
    assert hi+u-hi == u
# The two high raw significands and the nonzero low significand are odd.
assert (0x3ff0000000000001 & 1) == (0x0030000000000001 & 1) == 1
assert (0 & 1, 1 & 1) == (0, 1)  # proposed common low comparison selects zero
# Both-low-zero subnormal neighbours still use high parity.
assert f64(2)-f64(1) == u and (1 & 1, 2 & 1) == (1, 0)
print('PASS: independent exact PPC FMA/conversion midpoints and parity distinction')

spec = (ROOT / 'spec/Bennett-ISA-Spec-0.9.md').read_text()
block = spec.split('**CLAIM-REPLAY witness**', 1)[1].split('```json\n', 1)[1].split('\n```', 1)[0]
assert block.count('"borrowed":["1"]') == 6
fixed = block.replace('"borrowed":["1"]', '"borrowed":[]')
fixed = fixed.replace('"borrowed":[1]', '"borrowed":[]')
fixed = fixed.replace('1/33554432', '1/16777216')
k13 = json.loads(fixed)
tuples = [k13['row']['before'], k13['row']['after'],
          k13['wrong_row']['before'], k13['wrong_row']['after'],
          k13['changed_arg_after'], k13['short_budget_after']]
assert all(t['borrowed'] == [] and t['regions'] == [['2','1']] for t in tuples)
assert k13['widths']['borrowed'] == []
bits = sum(k13['widths']['bundles']) + sum(sum(r) for r in k13['widths']['regions'])
assert bits == 24 and str(F(1, 1 << bits)) == k13['covered_fraction']
assert 2 ^ ((1+2) % 4) == 1 and 2 ^ ((1+1) % 4) == 0
assert (2 ^ 0, 3 ^ 1) == (2, 2)
assert k13['sample_count'] == 4 and k13['distinct_samples'] == 1
print('PASS: all six K13 tuple repairs; 24-bit domain and exact singleton coverage')

# A finite coverage counterexample, not an implementation of the checker.
minimum = (3,0,0,0)
def covers(tk, arg, domain=range(4), token_arg=None):
    return (all(a <= b for a,b in zip(minimum,tk)) and arg in domain
            and (token_arg is None or token_arg == (3,0,0,0)))
assert covers((3,0,0,0),1) and covers((9,0,0,0),1)
assert not covers((2,0,0,0),1)
assert not covers((3,0,0,0),3,range(3))
assert not covers((9,0,0,0),1,token_arg=(4,0,0,0))
contexts = [(3,0,0,0), (2,0,0,0)]
assert [covers(t,1) for t in contexts] == [True,False]
assert {('G','H'), ('H','G')} == {('H','G'), ('G','H')}
print('PASS: qinplace minimum, exact-token, args-domain and two-context counterexamples')

# Proposed wire-request example; arithmetic is independent of any runtime body.
mask = (1 << 257)-1
a, b = (1 << 256)+3, 2
assert ((a*b) & mask) == 6
assert a.bit_count() == 3 and (((a*b) & mask).bit_count()) == 2
assert [min(64,257-64*k) for k in range(5)] == [64,64,64,64,1]
entries = [{'op':'mul','width':257}, {'op':'ctpop','width':257}, {'op':'mul','width':257}]
canonical_entries = [{'op':op,'width':w} for op,w in sorted({(e['op'],e['width']) for e in entries})]
assert canonical_entries == [{'op':'ctpop','width':257},{'op':'mul','width':257}]
request = {'schema':'bennett-runtimes-instantiations-1', 'llvm_major':18,
           'target_triple':'x86_64-unknown-linux-musl', 'data_layout':'e-p:64:64',
           'entries':canonical_entries}
canonical = json.dumps(request,sort_keys=True,separators=(',',':')).encode()
print('Proposed request example SHA-256:',hashlib.sha256(canonical).hexdigest())
print('PASS: i257 top-limb arithmetic and canonical request example')

def run(argv, cwd=ROOT, timeout=20, expected=0):
    p = subprocess.run([str(x) for x in argv], cwd=cwd, text=True,
                       capture_output=True, timeout=timeout)
    assert p.returncode == expected, (argv,p.returncode,p.stdout,p.stderr)
    return p
native = run([sys.executable,'-I','-B','-u',WS/'bennett-arith/tests/ppc_zero_low_witness.py'])
assert native.stdout.count('flags=0;') == 2
assert native.stdout.count('flags=24;') == 2
print(native.stdout, end='')
tests = run([sys.executable,'-B','-m','unittest','-v',
             'tests.test_replay.K13Witness','tests.test_replay.QinplaceCoverage'],cwd=REPLAY)
assert 'Ran 12 tests' in tests.stderr and tests.stderr.rstrip().endswith('OK')
print('PASS: independent replay focused tests, 12/12')
runtime = WS/'bennett-runtimes'
ll = runtime/'.tmp/sol-runtimes-queue/tmp/runtime-width-request.ll'
for major in (18,23):
    tools = Path(f'/usr/lib/llvm-{major}/bin')
    run([tools/'opt','-passes=verify','-disable-output',ll])

    assert not {'__bennett_rt_i257_mul','__bennett_rt_i257_ctpop'} & defs
    print('PASS: LLVM',major,'i257 source verifies; both definitions absent;',
          'runtime SHA-256',hashlib.sha256(bc.read_bytes()).hexdigest())

replay = json.loads(run([sys.executable,'-B','-m','checker_replay',
                        WITNESS/'certificate.json','--object',WITNESS/'object.bisa',
                        '--max-elements','16'],cwd=REPLAY).stdout)

original = run([REF,'check',WITNESS/'object.bisa'],expected=1)
assert 'c must name one wire' in original.stderr
scratch = JOB/'tmp/mcx-ccx.bisa'
source = (WITNESS/'object.bisa').read_text()
old = 'e38d5da64f9a0ae89a66243bcbe5a321cb807a5bbabf83952b63d3465dfab346'
new = 'f53238499e07824b6c62385c33426d4bc56bfaf900df61264ec65e0589061f39'
assert source.count('mcx (c) p') == 1 and source.count(old) == 1

for command in ('check','hash'):
    result = json.loads(run([REF,command,scratch]).stdout)
    assert result['object_hash'] == 'c785ff3f310f968e2a26f68834e99ab892cf0d1bffe816cf26b17d45c33a65af'
    assert result['symbols']['a']['interface_hash'] == new
    assert result['symbols']['a']['closure_hash'] == new

    result = json.loads(run([REF,'run-action',scratch,'a','--mut',f'{p},{q}','--in',str(c)]).stdout)
    assert result['mut'] == [q ^ int(c==3),p] and result['in'] == [c]
    back = json.loads(run([REF,'run-action',scratch,'a','--mut',
                          ','.join(map(str,result['mut'])),'--in',str(c),'--adjoint']).stdout)
    assert back['mut'] == [p,q] and back['in'] == [c]
print('PASS: mcx replay proof; original reference rejection; ccx check/hash and 16+16 executions')
print('PASS: derive3 verification complete; proposals remain unaccepted/unimplemented')

## Remaining decisions and ownership

Council/editor owners must ratify and publish these changes; the existing
five issues remain their tracking records. Arith still owes exact incoming
conversion/FMA mechanics and flags after the parity decision. Runtimes
and driver/plugin still owe the helper protocol and bodies. Checker and
executor owners must add the proposed context/operand vectors, and the
K13 editor must publish a complete corrected fixture. None of that
downstream implementation is claimed complete by this notes-only job.
# spec/

`Bennett-ISA-Spec.md` is the normative text: draft 0.8, **accepted by the
three-model council on 2026-10-28 (round 8, all members accept, no blocking
issues, mean 4.17/5)**. Council records and the runner live in the `bennett`
meta repo (`docs/council/isa-2026-10-28/`, `tools/council.py`).

Rules for changing it:

- Every semantic change bumps the full version string (section 1); drafts
  are not envelope-compatible, so the four example hashes in section 16.3
  change with it. Run `python3 reference-interpreter/isa_check.py --repatch`
  then `python3 reference-interpreter/isa_check.py`.
- Surgical edits only; a rewrite has introduced a mechanical error every
  time it was tried.
- Add every new example or stated result to `isa_check.py` before anyone
  grades it.
- Section 17 lists decisions deferred to implementation with their
  defaults; an implementer who must choose something not covered there
  files an issue and the default goes into section 17 first.
- Revisions go back through the council.

## 1. Scope and conformance

### 1.1 What the ISA is

A program model for classical, reversible and coherent computation with
one semantics, executed by the VM (`bennett-vm`) and the circuit
specialiser (`bennett-circuits`), produced by the plugin
(`bennett-plugin`) through the dialect's reference semantics, scheduled
through §11, described for hardware by target descriptions (§8). Nothing
here refers to LLVM.

### 1.2 Conformance classes

| Class | Who | Obligation |
|---|---|---|
| Executor | VM, specialiser, hardware stacks | §3–§7, §14 exactly, and the checking of inverse-pair claims (§4.5, §12.1; a certificate applies when its `closure_hash` binding equals the region's); suites A–E, H |
| Producer | plugin, library compilers | well-formed objects (§9, §16) and regions (§5, §6); suite F |
| Scheduler | `bennett-sched` and competitors | §11; only schedules valid under §6; suite H |
| Checker | `bennett-conformance` | validates certificates (§12) without trusting producers |
| Target author | vendors | target descriptions (§8); suite E |

### 1.3 Deferred *(asserted non-semantic)*

Binary encodings (§16 text is the interchange form); mechanisation of
§14; device models beyond §7.3; the gate-name registry process; the
memory layout of the opaque C objects of §10–§11 (every constructor,
accessor and destructor is listed). **Minimality claims** (north star
§5) are not supported by cert 0.9 and MUST NOT appear in a certificate.
Decisions deferred to implementation, each with its default, are listed
in §17.

## 2. Terminology

- **Tier.** CC, R, Q. **Wire**, **bundle** `wire[N]` (little-endian).
- **Location** `reg[N]`: a named mutable cell of an oracle or lproc
  register file. **Pseudo-location**: an oracle interface input or
  classical argument, or an lproc classical parameter (§3.7), readable
  only in its declaring function, never written.
- **Region**: a Q function with a declared interface: **lifted oracle**
  (§5.2) or **quantum action** (§5.3); **permutation action** (§5.3).
- **lproc**: a lifted procedure with its own params and locations; it
  resolves names only to its own params and locations (§3.7).
- **Budget**: classical (B, heap_limit, stack_limit, depth_limit).
- **Frame**: per machine step: `old_pc`, slots (assignment, capture and
  delivery slots, §3.6), `flag`, `succ`.
- **Basis configuration**: classical values on every component of D.
- **Gate classes.** Permutation: `x cx ccx mcx swap`. Diagonal: `s sdg t
  tdg cz rz`. Non-diagonal: `h rx ry`.
- **Verifying executor**: an executor that can decide exactly whether a
  set of wires is clean on every basis configuration (a state-vector or
  branch-enumeration simulator); every other executor (hardware) is
  **non-verifying**. Rules that depend on the difference say which
  applies (§3.3 `qreg.free`, §10, §17 item 14).
- **Effective control context** at a point: the set of wires on the
  active predicate stack plus the inherited control context (the control
  wires of every enclosing `ctl` and predicate stack of every enclosing
  invocation, transitively); duplicates count once. Its size is the
  **effective control count**.

## 3. Machine model
## 9. Objects, identity, linking, reversible ABI (object 0.9)

### 9.1 Sections and schemas

Order fixed: `symbols imports functions data devices layout schedule
certificate coverage`; each at most once; unknown sections or fields are
errors. Metadata sections are one JCS line each, an object:

```
  symbols:  { "<local index>": { "name", "kind": "cc"|"r"|"oracle"|"action"|"perm_action"|"lproc"|"data", "signature": <sig>, "interface_hash" } }
  imports:  { "<name>": { "signature": <sig>, "pinned_closure_hash"?: hex } }
  devices:  { "models": { "<dev>": { "version": "0.9" } }, "services": { "<reverse-dns id>": { "version": "…" } } }
  layout:   { "ptr_width", "H", "A_tab", "S_cells", "null_size", "heap_base", "heap_limit_max",
              "globals": { "<name>": { "base", "size", "image": "<data name>" } },
              "interface_regions": { "<oracle>": { "<region>": { "base" } } }, "block_image"?: "<data name>" }
  schedule: §11      certificate: §12      coverage: { "entries": [ §13 ] }
  <sig>:    kind "oracle": { "tier":"q", "kind", "in":[w…], "tgt":w, "fault":F, "mem":[{"A","W","role"}], "args":[types], "abi":"0.9" }
            kind "action"/"perm_action": { "tier":"q", "kind", "mut":[w…], "in":[w…], "mem":[…], "args":[types], "bounds":[…], "abi" }
            kind "lproc": { "tier":"q", "kind", "params":[w…], "result":w, "tokens":["token"…], "args":[types], "mem":[{"A","W","role":"in"}], "abi" }
            kind "cc"/"r": { "tier", "kind", "params":[types], "results":[types], "abi" }
            kind "data": { "tier":"-", "kind", "size", "abi" }
```

Integers that may exceed 2^53 are decimal strings, and a JSON number ≥
2^53 anywhere in metadata is an error. `data` lines are `NAME SP HEX`.
**Encodings.** `types` in `args`, `params` and `results` are the §16.2
type spellings as JSON strings (`"i64"`, `"ptr"`, `"f64"`, `"token"`,
`"qreg[8]"`); `w` in `in`, `tgt`, `mut`, lproc `params` and `result` is
the integer bundle or location width (§16.3). An lproc's `tokens`,
`args` and `mem` arrays are mandatory (empty arrays when absent from the
header); `args` excludes tokens and `mem` has only `in` roles (§3.7).
Array order is header order within each group. A **program-point id**
(§9.2) is the JSON array `[interface_hash, block name, ordinal]`.
Certificate `frame` fields use exactly the typed-record schema of §9.4;
unknown fields are errors there as in every metadata record.
`devices.models` lists every §7.3 model that an `effect` of the object
uses (an `effect` on an unlisted §7.3 model is a load error; a device
outside §7.3 is never listed and faults at run time, §17 item 3; a key
that is not a §7.3 model is an unknown field); `devices.services` lists
every `hostcall` service (§10). An interface region's name (its param
name and its `layout.interface_regions.<oracle>` key) MUST differ from
every symbol and import name of the object, since `@name` in `lift.addr`
denotes either (§9.2).

### 9.2 Identity

An lproc's token, classical and region parameter groups are part of
its JCS signature (§9.1), including their order and types. The header
and every forwarded operand list are canonical function bytes; changing
them changes the envelope and, through references, the caller's closure
hash. Fixed binding instances are derived per oracle (§3.7), not new
symbols or separately hashed bodies; instance rows share the source
row's program-point id and expand once per fixed row instance per step,
while the instance and semantic-call occurrence components in §11
invocation paths and §12.1 context keys distinguish their uses. [deps: ISA-3.7-P5, ISA-3.7-P6, ISA-11.1-P9, ISA-12.1-P11] Entry argument values and token tuples bind invocations through
`params_hash`, not through the lproc's interface hash (this section).
Imports must match all five groups; validation and instance formation
are repeated after linking (§9.3).

**Canonical function bytes**: the text from the function's header line
through its last instruction line, lines joined by LF, no trailing LF.
**Semantic envelope** of a symbol (byte-exact; every part ends with LF):

```
  (1) "isa=" <full version string, e.g. 0.9>
  (2) "ptr_width=" INT
  (3) the JCS signature
  (4) the canonical function bytes (with reference tokens replaced, below); for a data symbol: its data line NAME SP HEX
  (5a) for every data entry in the closure set, sorted by name bytewise:        "data " NAME " " HEX
  (5b) for every layout leaf in the closure set, sorted by JCS path bytes: "layout " <JCS key-component array> " " <JCS value>. [deps: ISA-9.1, ISA-16.1]
  (5c) for an R/CC function, for every device model and service it uses, sorted: "device " <name> " " <JCS entry>
  (6) for every referenced symbol outside the symbol's SCC, in order of first reference: its 64-hex hash
```

**Closure set**, by symbol kind. **Oracle**: **all** `layout` leaves of
the object except `interface_regions` entries of other oracles (every
global's pre-entry affects its allocation faults), its own `interface_
regions.<oracle>.*` leaves, and every data image those leaves name.
**Lproc**: the leaves and images of every global `@g` it names in `lift.
addr`. **Action / permutation action**: no layout leaves; the `@image`
of each `qregion` is an ordinary symbol reference (replaced in part (4),
listed in part (6)). **CC / R function**: all `layout` leaves, every
data image they name, and in (5c) every device model and `hostcall`
service it uses. **Data symbol**: parts (5)–(6) are empty. A layout leaf
is a scalar or an object at the deepest level the schema lists (`globals.
<name>` and `interface_regions.<oracle>.<region>` are leaves; `ptr_
width`, `H`, … are leaves); a scalar's JCS value is its decimal text.
**Globals are data symbols**: every global `g` in `layout.globals` MUST
have `image` = `g` and the object MUST define a `data` symbol named `g`
of that size, so the token `@g` in `lift.addr` is both the global and a
symbol reference (replaced by the data symbol's hash; the global's
`base` and `size` enter through the layout leaf). An `@name` naming an
interface region (in `lift.addr`) is not a symbol reference and is left
unchanged. **Reference replacement**: in parts
(4)–(5) every `@name` symbol-reference token is replaced by `@` followed
by the referenced symbol's 64-hex hash (the symbol's own name in its
header line is a definition, not a reference, and is left unchanged); the **interface hash** uses
interface hashes of defined symbols and `SHA-256(name ‖ 0x00 ‖ JCS
signature)` (hex) for unresolved imports; the **closure hash** uses
closure hashes. **Acyclic and cyclic symbols.** The reference graph
has an edge from a symbol to every symbol it references (parts
(4)–(5)). An **acyclic** symbol — a one-member SCC that does not
reference itself — has hash = SHA-256 of its own envelope (example 1's
`inc8`, example 2's `zero` and `sum_db`); the SCC procedure below applies
to every **cyclic** SCC, including a one-member SCC whose function
references itself (example 2's `sum`). **SCCs**: members ordered by name bytewise; every intra-
SCC `@name` token (parts (4)–(5)) is replaced by `@$<decimal index>`;
intra-SCC references contribute no part-(6) line; `scc_hash` = SHA-256
over the concatenation of the members' envelopes in that order; member
hash = SHA-256 over the ASCII bytes of `<scc_hash hex>:<decimal index>`.
**object_hash** = SHA-256 of the canonical object text with the
`schedule`, `certificate` and `coverage` sections deleted together with
the blank line preceding each. `reference_program_hash` = the oracle's
interface hash. `params_hash` = SHA-256 of the JCS array of classical
parameter values (the typed encoding of §12.3, K13); `schedule_hash` =
SHA-256 of the schedule's JCS bytes.
**Program-point id** = `(symbol interface hash, block name, instruction
ordinal within the block counting nested lines in text order)`.
Executable certificates and caches bind to **closure hashes** (required,
§12).

A layout leaf's path is the JCS array of its original JSON key
components from the layout root to the deepest schema-listed leaf,
so a scalar has a one-component path and an interface-region leaf has
path `["interface_regions","<oracle>","<region>"]`.
[deps: ISA-9.1-P1, ISA-16.1]
Part (5b) emits exactly one line for each structured leaf in the closure
set, including distinct leaves whose dotted renderings coincide, ordered
by the unsigned UTF-8 bytes of that JCS path array, independently of the
leaf values; no dotted rendering is used as a key or an ordering key.
[deps: ISA-9.1-P1, ISA-16.1]
An oracle's own interface-region leaves and entries of other oracles
are selected by equality of the original JSON key under
`interface_regions` to the oracle's name, never by a prefix of a
rendered path; an lproc's global leaves are selected by equality of
the key under `globals` to the global name.
[deps: ISA-3.7-P1, ISA-9.1-P1]

**Envelope boundary lemma** (non-normative).
For a function symbol, §16.2 requires a header beginning `fn `,
`oracle `, `action `, `perm_action ` or `lproc `; following lines are
`regs`, block labels, instructions, or structured closing braces. [deps: ISA-16.2]
No §4 opcode is `data`, `layout` or `device`; a result with one of
those names has ` = ` after the name, which cannot begin any part-(5)
line (a data name, a JSON array, or a device name is required there). [deps: ISA-4.8, ISA-9.1, ISA-16.2]
No canonical function line is a bare 64-hex string, so the first
part-(5)/(6) line ends the function bytes unambiguously. [deps: ISA-4.8, ISA-16.2]
The fixed `data `, `layout `, `device ` prefixes distinguish (5a–c),
and a bare 64-hex line distinguishes (6); JSON strings escape LF. [deps: ISA-9.1, ISA-16.1]
For a data symbol, the signature kind selects exactly one data line
for (4), with (5)–(6) empty, even when the data name itself is `data`,
`layout` or `device`. [deps: ISA-9.1, ISA-16.2]
Together with the fixed first three lines and the injective JCS array
paths, these facts make the envelope parts recoverable without length
prefixes; this is an encoding argument, not a claim of SHA-256 injectivity. [deps: ISA-9.1, ISA-16.1, ISA-16.2, ISA-9.2-P4]

### 9.3 Linking

Imports resolve by name + signature (mismatch: link error; pinned hash:
equality required); resolved closure hashes are recorded in the linked
certificate. Equal-closure-hash duplicates deduplicate; unequal under one
name, or conflicting `devices`/`layout`/`data` entries: link error.
Linking = resolution, validation, certificate composition (§12.3),
rebinding to closure hashes.

### 9.4 Reversible ABI

- CC/R calls: classical frame; callee frames follow the call boundary;
  `uncall` reverses segments (§7.4).
- Region calls: interface bundles and regions passed in declared order,
  caller-owned; callee owns its workspace, returns it clean, local regions
  at image, borrowed bundles returned.
- **Frame descriptor** (certificate): interface slots retain their identities;
  scratch is recorded by class and cleanliness, with widths as expressions
  in inherited control count `c`; retained history is B × frame width for
  the full-history oracle expansion, otherwise the bound library's realised
  history (§17 item 18). Borrowed wires and scheme ancilla frames are
  separate records. Callees `qborrow` only from the caller's designated
  idle set. Shared frames require disjoint live ranges (suite F).

**Frame JSON schema (B5).** The descriptor is the following JCS object;
all five arrays are required, including empty arrays. Every record has
exactly the shown fields; `live_ranges` is optional. Unknown fields at
any depth are errors under §9.1.

```
  { "slots": [ { "id": "<slot identity>", "role": "in"|"tgt"|"fault"|"mut"|"mem_in"|"mem_mut", "class": "<cls>", "width": expr }, … ],
    "scratch": [ { "id": "<frame identity>", "class": "<cls>", "state": "clean"|"dirty", "width": expr }, … ],
    "history": [ { "id": "<frame identity>", "class": "<cls>", "width": expr }, … ],
    "borrow": [ { "id": "<frame identity>", "class": "<cls>", "width": expr }, … ],
    "ancillae": [ { "id": "<frame identity>", "scheme": "<scheme name>", "class": "<cls>", "state": "clean"|"dirty", "width": expr }, … ],
    "live_ranges"?: [ { "frame": "<frame identity>", "start": ordinal, "end": ordinal }, … ] }
```

`slots` lists the source interface in operand order: oracle `in`, `tgt`,
`fault`, then regions; action `mut`, `in`, then regions. Slot identities
are `<group>:<zero-based index within group>` (`in:0`, `tgt:0`,
`fault:0`, `mut:0`, `mem:0`, …); a region uses role `mem_in` or
`mem_mut`. A region slot's width counts all its cells, in address then
bit order; bundle bits are least-significant first. Slot widths MUST
match the signature. Inherited controls are caller-owned and precede
these slots in §11 topology placement; they are not renamed callee
slots. Every class is a resolved target class. Non-slot frame identities
are nonempty producer-chosen strings, distinct from every other record
identity in the descriptor; a dynamic identity additionally includes
the invocation context and the expansion scratch identity (§11.1).
Array order is source interface order for slots, and first allocation
order in the realised expansion for each other array. `scratch` records
per-class, per-cleanliness workspace; `history` records retained history;
`borrow` records dirty requirements; `ancillae` records each scheme's
ancilla frame and its declared state. These categories do not double
count the same wires. No requirements may be omitted because they vanish
for one chosen basis input.

Every `expr` uses §8.1's expression language with **only `c` bound**;
other variables and table lookups (whose indices require `k` or `eps`)
are invalid. At every covered inherited control count the widths MUST
be nonnegative integers; interface widths are positive and signature
fixed. Instantiation substitutes the resolved budget, parameters and
expansion choices into constants before forming these expressions.
Widths describe the actual realisation, including history and scheme
ancillae, and are checked against it. Each `live_ranges` entry names a
non-slot frame identity and a half-open interval `[start,end)` of its
invocation's generated leaf order (including step/phase/path expansion,
§11); `start < end`. A shared frame identity retains its recorded class, state and width
across uses; every use of shared storage MUST be listed and intervals
of the same frame MUST be pairwise disjoint. An omitted list gives no shared-storage permission.

**FRAME witness**

```json
{"ancillae":[{"class":"fast","id":"a","scheme":"controlled-x","state":"clean","width":{"var":"c"}}],"borrow":[{"class":"fast","id":"b","width":1}],"history":[{"class":"fast","id":"h","width":3}],"live_ranges":[{"end":2,"frame":"s","start":0},{"end":4,"frame":"s","start":2}],"scratch":[{"class":"fast","id":"s","state":"clean","width":{"args":[2,{"var":"c"}],"op":"+"}}],"slots":[{"class":"fast","id":"mut:0","role":"mut","width":2},{"class":"fast","id":"in:0","role":"in","width":1}]}
```

At `c = 2`, the slot widths total 3, scratch width is 4, retained
history width is 3, borrow width is 1 and scheme ancilla width is 2.
The two half-open uses of `s` meet at 2 and do not overlap. This is a
schema/arithmetic witness; a real certificate must also bind the
expansion and target defining `controlled-x`. Its canonical UTF-8
bytes exclude the code-fence LF.
Frame SHA-256: `f314eeef24a5a65a9c72f91e062aa3c83556358eaca166e689571700a72083ff`.

Subtraction adds bitwise NOT b with c[0]=1. Unsigned a<b is NOT the final subtraction carry; equality is the AND of NOT(a[i] XOR b[i]) in increasing-bit left-associated order. `ule` is OR(ult,eq); signed comparison selects sign(a) if signs differ, else ult. | §17 item 7: "ripple-carry adders and comparators"; §4.1: "wrap"; §3.7: prospective sums use "no wrap" (retain the extra carry for those). |

Multiply folds shifted partial products in increasing multiplier-bit order through ripple add modulo 2^N. Shifts scan shift amounts j=0..N−1, equality-selecting the corresponding source bit; ≥N produces zero. Sign-extend replicates the high source bit, zero-extend supplies zero, truncate drops high bits.
